| 2.3 | VocabEntry table + weekly mining pass (cron): cluster unknowns, promote to shadow→flagged→active per the audit §4.2 loop-2 gates (held-out replay before promotion). Routing-semantics change ⇒ critic. | Opus 4.8 build; **Fable 5 critic** | high | 100k + 30k | [x] done 2026-07-12 (#225/#226; built + criticized on Fable). Additive `VocabEntry` (per-user, `@@unique([userId,phrase])`) + pure `engine/vocab/vocab.ts` (`normalizePhrase`/`matchVocab`/`mineVocab`) + Prisma-only `server/vocab.ts` + `/api/cron/vocab` (Mon 16:00). **An entry supplies an intent KIND and nothing else** — every parameter is re-derived from the asker's own words via `intentFromKind` + `validateIntent`, the same contract the LLM classifier has had since #75 — so the layer is exactly as powerful as the model route it replaces, minus the model call, and keeps working with NO provider key. Order: parser → frame → vocab → LLM, on a parser-`unknown` only. Ladder: shadow (≥3 unanimous INDEPENDENT rescues, not served) → flagged (≥2 held-out agreements, served + hedged) → active (≥2 disclosed serves) → retired (terminal). Self-confirmation impossible: `vocab:`-tagged rows count only as `served`, never evidence; `frame:` rows make a phrase permanently ineligible. Undo on the answer and on /settings, terminal. **Deviation from the audit, recorded:** per-user vocabulary, not cross-user n-gram clusters (a scrubbed phrase is not an anonymized one; a whole-question key cannot fire on a question it was never mined from). Three parallel fresh-context Fable critics: cycle 1 FAIL — 0 P0, **5 P1**, 11 P2, all P1s + actionable P2s fixed + regression-locked (7 ledger entries): the shared DEMO account learned (a visitor's typed words would render in the next visitor's settings); a "Forget this" landing mid-mining-run was silently reverted; a served entry was unmonitorable and self-promoted (fixed by a weekly independent re-check — constitution (e)); `VocabEntry` was undisclosed in PRIVACY; and a PRE-EXISTING cardinal-sin parser bug ("spend at 星巴克" → the ALL-spending total, unhedged). | 2.6 | **Ask parser: make `spend_total` earn its answer (the spend-family sink).** Opened by the #226 critic cycles (4-cycle cap reached → escalated; see docs/STATUS.md §OPEN). `spend_total` is the unconditional fall-through of the spending family, and every guard in front of it is scoped to one sentence shape — so a FRONTED object ("At Costco, how much did I spend?") still answers the user's ENTIRE spending for a question about one store, unhedged. Cardinal sin, plain ASCII, pre-dates the vocab work. Fix = the inversion: require a POSITIVE licence (no unconsumed at/with/on object anywhere) before `spend_total` may be returned by the parser, the frame, OR `intentFromKind`. Bundle the smaller siblings: "at home depot" → the "home" GROUP (rent+mortgage in a one-retailer figure, #111-era substring fallback); "at - costco" / "at... costco" punctuation mangling; a non-ASCII custom-category name is unreachable. **Money-display routing ⇒ Fable critic.** | **Fable 5 (high)** build + hostile critic | high | 80k + 30k | [x] done 2026-07-12 (#229, built + criticized on Fable). The inversion shipped as ONE shared primitive: `unconsumedSpendObject` — every at/with/on/in object anywhere in the question must be consumed-class (timeframe/total cues, a conservative idiom set, question machinery, month-adjacent digits) up to a closer, or `spend_total` is refused by the parser sink, `intentFromKind` (LLM + vocab routes), and the frame alike. Siblings: home depot/homegoods/home-and-garden → merchant (tier-3 fallback word-bounded + extension-checked); punctuation glue tolerated ("at - costco"/"at... costco" → merchant "costco"); custom "Café" reachable via NFC/Unicode-boundary matching + EXACT-object carve-outs (tail included); frame BLOCKS refused objects ("with amex/income in june" no longer answer the carried question's window swap; pronouns still carry). Fresh-context Fable critic cycle 1 FAIL — 2 P0 (one licensed word licensed the whole object: "at Best Buy…" still took the total), 1 P1 (frame on/for silent drop), 2 P2, 2 P3, ALL fixed; cycle 2 PASS — all 7 closed by re-executed repros, 0 P0/P1, 2 new P2s also fixed ("Do It Best", carve-out prefix-equality). | 4.6 | **Cross-provider account reconciliation** (owner question 2026-07-22; spec `docs/PROVIDER_RECONCILIATION_ARCHITECTURE.md`, DECISIONS #266). Elevated by the live SimpleFIN→Plaid migration: today, connecting Plaid on top of the old (disconnected-but-retained) SimpleFIN accounts double-counts BOTH balances (net worth sums every row, no staleness filter) AND overlapping transactions (dedup is per `(accountId, providerRef)`), and the only resolution — deleting the stale side — discards its pre-Plaid history + corrections. Shared `isAccountLive` helper = the SAME liveness the slice-5 caller will feed the detector. 20 real-Prisma tests. | L.9 | **The Continue-an-account card shows two non-comparable account numbers and offers the SAME predecessor against TWO different successors — one of them the WRONG retirement account.** Owner-reported 2026-07-24 with a /accounts screenshot, verbatim: *"simplefin and schwab account # hard to read and don't match, even though i know they are same account."* The card offered `Charles Schwab US Roth Contributory IRA ...396 (396)` (SimpleFIN, the predecessor) → `Michael Lee - Roth IRA Brokerage Account - ****5351` (Plaid) at confidence "possible" on `shared name: "ira", "roth"`, AND the SAME SimpleFIN row → `Michael Lee - Traditional IRA Brokerage Account - ****1548` at "possible" on `shared name: "ira"` alone. **Five verified causes, all read this session:** (1) **SimpleFIN never writes `Account.mask`** — `simplefin.ts:474-478` (create) and `:487` (update) write providerRef/name/type/balance/currency and no mask — so `masksDiffer` (`duplicates.ts:172`, which reads the mask COLUMN only) is **structurally always false for every SimpleFIN↔Plaid pair**, and the #291/#292 "a differing last-4 vetoes the name signal" guard **cannot fire on the exact cross-provider pairing it is most needed for**; (2) Schwab's SimpleFIN identifier is **three digits (396)** while the whole last-4 machinery hard-codes four — `maskFromName` (`duplicates.ts:111-117`) matches only `\((\d{4})\)` or a mask-prefixed `\d{4}`, so "…396 (396)" yields **null** and is invisible to the positive match too; (3) with no mask signal in either direction the WEAK name signal alone licenses the pair (`duplicates.ts:200-204`), and **neither "ira" nor "roth" is in `NAME_STOPWORDS`** (`duplicates.ts:65-93` — verified by direct read; a Haiku explorer this session hallucinated "ira" at line 88, which is actually `'simplefin'`), so one generic retirement-account word proposes merging a Roth into a Traditional; (4) `detectReconciliationCandidates` (`duplicates.ts:374-395`) is a bare O(n²) pair loop with **no dedupe by predecessor**, so one stale row is offered against every successor it matches, each badged identically; (5) the label is `{name} ({providerMask})` (`accounts-list.tsx:718-724` + `duplicate-card-view.ts:46-48`) printed over names that ALREADY embed the number the bank supplied — hence "…IRA ...396 (396)" and "- ****5351 (Plaid ····5351)" — so the number is doubled on one side and the two sides show numbers that were never comparable in the first place. **Risk = P1, money-visible and mis-directed:** the two proposals look equally plausible, and tapping Combine on the wrong one folds a Roth IRA's history into a Traditional IRA. BOTH REPRODUCED FIRST, then fixed** (the repro run's real output is in PROGRESS.md: (a) returned `kind:'linked'` with 2 items where the card was already reachable; (b) two concurrent exchanges left 2 items and 2 rows for one card). **(a)** `classifyNewItem` no longer reads a null `institutionId` as "different bank" — it reads it as "this row has never been asked". A candidate that cannot name its bank is asked over the wire (`/item/get`) and the answer is written back exactly as the `syncInstitutions` sweep would, so the id is bought at most once per connection, only while a link at that user's other bank is in flight, and never again. A candidate that cannot answer takes part in no match and the new link is kept — the same abstention direction as the rest of the door. Chosen over "leave it to the sweep and say so": a disclosure that the protection is off is not protection, and the on-demand call is bounded and self-healing. **(b)** Reproduced with two real `exchangePublicToken` calls in one `Promise.all`, which is exactly the two-tab shape: both saw zero connections and both persisted. Fixed by a lease, `PlaidLinkClaim @@unique([userId, institutionId])`, held across the classify-and-write window and released in a `finally`; the loser waits, then sees the winner's connection and treats its own link as the refresh it is. Deliberately NOT a unique constraint on `PlaidItem` — two connections at one bank are legitimate (a spouse's login), so the DECISION is what must be exclusive, not the outcome. Expiry + takeover so a request that dies cannot wall off a bank, and the takeover deletes by the id it just read so a rival cannot delete the live claim its winner made. A 4s wait that PROCEEDS UNPROTECTED on timeout, because timing out the exchange would orphan a billed Item whose token was never stored (#307's worst case) while proceeding yields at worst a duplicate #306 discloses and #304 combines. 10 new tests, 5 of them locks on the lease's own failure modes (abandoned lease, throwing link, cross-bank, cross-user). Fix is a 32ms drain in `tests/e2e/helpers/test.ts` doing what React's own rAF callback would do; fence held by construction (one module, `browser.newContext` patched, eslint `no-restricted-imports` across all 56 spec files); locked by `tests/e2e/streamed-reveal.spec.ts` with two anti-vacuity guards, mutation-proven. No locator loosened, no spec retitled. **Historical record of the diagnosis follows:** The stated hypothesis is **killed**: it is not write contention and nothing is missing from the page — the seeded card renders *twice*. Cause, read out of the failing response body that Playwright's `retain-on-failure` trace had stored all along (`trace.zip → resources/<sha1>.html`): React streams resolved Suspense content into `div#S:0[hidden]` and emits `$RC("B:0","S:0")` to move it into place, but the shipped `$RC` does not move anything inline — it queues the pair on `$RB` and defers the reveal to `$RV` behind `requestAnimationFrame`. Starve the renderer of frames and the staged copy is never moved while React's client render fills `#content` from the RSC payload, so the page holds the content twice. That explains every previously unexplained fact: no hydration error (nothing mismatched), the duplicate persisting a full 20s (the frame is absent, not late), `load` firing with the content still staged (the reveal is deferred by design), and the a11y snapshot looking clean (`hidden` prunes it). The orphan sits **outside `#content` and after it in document order**, so no reader, screen reader or `getElementById` can reach it — production cost is one wasted DOM copy, not a correctness risk. **Three candidate fixes were executed and killed, so nobody re-buys them:** deleting `(app)/loading.tsx` (the obvious structural fix — A/B'd with a forced 1500ms delay and a rebuild; the staging container was still present 6/6, so it would have reversed product decision #81 for nothing); the three Chromium backgrounding flags (Playwright already passes all three by default — measured no-op, reverted); and any isolated reproduction (the 4 specs pass 18/18 alone, a forced-slow page 4/4 at `--workers=1`, and an instrumented probe under CPU throttling, network throttling and 4-way concurrency gave **zero duplications in 96 loads** — while full-suite runs on the same tree failed 5, then 1, then 6). **Next:** read `$RV`/`$RB` batching in `react-dom@19.1.0`'s shipped bundle — `$RC` schedules only when `2===$RB.length`, so a push landing while `$RV` drains (it ends `a.length=0`) never schedules its own reveal and orphans that boundary permanently; if that is it, the fix is a react-dom upgrade, not app code. Any candidate must be measured against FULL-suite runs. Do not loosen the locators — a real duplicate render puts both copies inside `#content` — and note that scoping to `#content` is not a per-spec change: 19 spec files load /accounts and the four failing ones hold 121 locator call sites, so it must be central or it will rot. See STATUS §"The mechanism, read out of the shipped React" + `docs/lessons/the-evidence-was-in-the-trace.md`. |
| O.4 | **"Please put standard login procedures for this app. The password appears persistent despite shutting down computer. This is dangerous."** (Owner, 2026-07-27.) Confirmed and fixed. `src/auth.config.ts` set `session: { strategy: 'jwt' }` with no `maxAge`, so Auth.js's **30-DAY** default (`@auth/core/lib/init.js:38`) governed both the JWT and the session cookie's `Expires` attribute — and a 30-day `Expires` is a *persistent* cookie the browser writes to disk, so browser-close and power-off both preserved the sign-in for a month. | **Fable 5** | small | 25k | **[x] DONE 2026-07-27 (DECISIONS #321).** One-line fix — `maxAge: SESSION_IDLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (30 min) — but the number was only safe to choose after verifying in the installed dependency source that it behaves as a ROLLING IDLE timeout: the `jwt` branch of `@auth/core/lib/actions/session.js` re-signs and re-sets the cookie on every session read (unconditionally — `updateAge` is database-strategy-only, hence deliberately not set), and `next-auth/lib/index.js` forwards those Set-Cookie headers out of middleware, which matches every app route. So active use never signs you out; 30 minutes of nothing does. Ships with the sign-in page stating the policy (minutes derived from the same constant, so copy and cookie cannot drift), `docs/LOGIN_AND_SESSIONS.md` as the written procedure (sign-in methods, sign-out, sign-out-everywhere, shared-computer guidance), a PRIVACY.md security-measures line, and `tests/unit/session-timeout.test.ts` bounding the window to 5–30 min (mutation-proven fail-old). Playwright exposure checked first: no spec uses `storageState`, 60 s per-test budget. **Deliberately not built:** an absolute session cap (can eject an active user mid-task for little gain over an idle timeout) and a "remember this device" opt-in (not expressible via Auth.js cookie config — the callsite hardcodes `expires` and spreads it over the configured options, so it needs a custom cookie writer). A pre-expiry warning modal is the natural follow-up if 30 min proves too short in practice. |

SimpleFIN's is the narrower "hold drifts past the 5-day overlap then re-posts under a new id" class, ageing out at `PENDING_MAX_AGE_DAYS = 32`. Both self-heal without user action, both were accepted after their own critic cycles (#128/#148), both fail in the safe direction, and both candidate fixes are rejected in the source with reasons. O.7 extends the exposure to one more READ surface, and the new pending disclosure is what gives a reader seeing a doubled figure the thread to pull. Two tables so "listing never loads a file" holds by construction. Upload is a ROUTE (Server Actions cap at 1 MB; a phone receipt exceeds it) with a hand-written CSRF check, and deliberately no `withDeadline` — that 8s deadline is for severed action streams and would false-alarm a healthy 5 MB phone upload. Owner-only, never a household partner (a receipt carries a name, an address, a prescription). One of the two proposed destinations turned out not to exist: `Category.parentId` is declared but has NEVER been populated (two-level taxonomy per #63/#65) and `group` is a plain string, not an assignable row — there is no parent category to route to, so building that option means inventing a category per group. The remaining option, `uncategorized`, fails in the worse direction: it discards what the engine knew, asserts a false "we don't know", fills the triage queue as a direct result of the reader asking to SEE LESS, and merges two meanings of the needs-review class ("could not tell" vs "you removed the bucket") across triage, the self-audit unknown-rate metric, the nudge feed and the AI-trust panel. | O.20f | **The O.20d controls are below the repo's own tap-target floor — two of the three fresh-context critics found it independently, on different surfaces.** Making the bars controls is what created the violation: an allocation segment's BUTTON is the painted bar (`allocation-drilldown.tsx` — `h-2.5` = 10px tall, width = the position's weight), so the demo's NVDA segment is a **34×10px** target at 380px, and a portfolio priced at 0 (`addHolding` accepts `priceCents === 0`) renders every segment `width: 0%` — **no allocation panel is openable by touch at all**. The retirement strip is worse by count: 56 bars (up to 103 at `currentAge 18 / endAge 120`) share ~316px ⇒ **≈4.7px each**, and a mis-tap returns the WRONG refusal on the WRONG bar. WCAG 2.2 AA **2.5.8** requires 24×24 and the repo's own `.tap-target` token (`globals.css`, gated by `tests/e2e/tap-targets.spec.ts`) sets 44px; none of the five new controls carries it. The e2e missed it because Playwright's `.click()` hits an element's center at any size, and the mobile-380 gate measures two hand-picked controls. Fix: make the allocation LEGEND entries the buttons (they already carry symbol + swatch + index) keeping the bar as the picture; give the retirement strip `overflow-x-auto` with a 24px minimum bar width; then extend `tap-targets.spec.ts` to enumerate all five O.20d controls so the gate can see the next one. **Carry these same-file P2s in the same pass, all from the O.20d-FU critics:** (a) the percent label's own stated rule ("a sub-0.5% position must not announce 0%") is only pushed one decimal — a $3.00 position in a $1,000,000.00 portfolio renders "AAPL 0.0%"; floor it to "<0.1"; (b) "whole percent when exact" is decided by IEEE754 residue, so an exact 50/29/14/7 split renders "50% · 29.0% · 14% · 7.0%" — decide on the integer side (`Math.round(v*10)`); (c) net-worth constituent rows arrive in database order, so the "Apr 30" and "Today" panels list the same accounts in different sequences — sort by name or signed balance; (d) the panel's inner Hide unmounts the panel and drops focus to `<body>` at all four call sites — hold a ref to the opening control and restore focus; (e) `key={c.accountId}` collides on the two-same-account-snapshots input the engine's own new test blesses; (f) the chip strip is unbounded (18 pills today on a 380px dashboard, one per snapshot ever) — cap to the chart's useful window with the live point always last; (g) the month-end basis sentence in `networth/panel.ts` documents itself as safe because "every snapshot writer today writes month-ends", which is FALSE of the writer it names — `seed/build.ts` writes the `back === 0` snapshot dated `asOf`. What actually protects it is an unrelated coincidence (`businessToday()` returns `DEFAULT_AS_OF` for the demo, so that row is always overwritten by the live point). Break the coincidence with a documented invocation — `npx prisma db seed -- --asOf 2026-05-15` while `DEMO_TODAY` stays `2026-06-10` — and a "May 15" chip opens a panel reading "the sum of every account's month-end balance on May 15, 2026". P2s, all to the row's prescription with the open choices recorded in DECISIONS #447: (a/b) new pure `allocationPercent` — "<0.1" for a sub-0.05% position, whole percents decided on `Math.round(v*10)` (50/29/14/7 renders whole), exactly zero renders "0", unit-locked; (c) constituents name-sorted in the engine's final map (accountId tiebreak — a balance sort would reorder panels when a reader re-files a row); (d) shared `usePanelToggleFocus` hook at ALL FIVE call sites (the row's "four" missed the creep chart, which is the fifth), e2e-asserted per surface that an inner-Hide restores focus to the opener; (e) keys `accountId:index`; (f) chip strip capped at the card's 18-month window, tail-slice keeps "Today" last, render-locked both sides of the boundary; (g) basis sentence derives from `isMonthEnd(date)` (derive, not drop — dropping the `back === 0` snapshot would change the seeded dataset), the seed docblock names the `--asOf 2026-05-15` invocation that makes the mid-month case reachable. | U.13 | **A genuine $2,086.40 deposit is silently dropped from every spending surface, today.** MEASURED, not inferred (`scripts/audit-probes/u11c-silent-loss-today.mts`, replaying the shipped `reconciliationTxnKeepFilter` over the owner's real corpus): of **709 rows the R1 rule drops, 706 are true duplicates** and **1 is a real "Deposit Mobile Banking" inflow on Investor Checking dated exactly the cutover**, which the predecessor never reported and which no surviving row replaces. It is absent from the register, budgets, reports and the tax export. This is the ordinary CHAIN rule, not the sibling shape: `txnKeepRule` drops any successor row inside the predecessor's claim span `[first, min(cutover,last)]` **whether or not the predecessor actually has that row** — and on the cutover day the two feeds legitimately differ, because that is the day one stopped and the other kept going. **Confirmed without the engine in the way** (`u11i-confirm-the-lost-deposit.mts`): the retired Schwab feed's LAST day is the cutover 2026-07-21 and it reported exactly one row that day (−$11.00 Venmo); the live Plaid feed reported that same Venmo **and** the +$2,086.40 deposit; the retired side holds no row of that amount on any date. So the boundary day is structurally the worst day to award by span — it is precisely the day the old feed's coverage ends mid-day and the new one's does not. It violates the failure direction this file states about itself at `reconcile-boundary.ts:329-331` ("a visible, advisory-covered double, never a silent loss"). Fix direction, evidence-backed: drop a row only when a counterpart is PROVEN on the claiming side — exact \|amount\| with a ±3-day tolerance, because the same probe caught one purchase posted 06-12 by one feed and 06-13 by the other (PGA TOUR SUPERSTORE, DICK'S). Blast radius is every `reconciliationTxnKeepFilter` caller. SHIPPED: the claim is half-open at both ends, `[first, claimEnd)`; the handover day is released to both sides, costing 9 rows / $374.40 of VISIBLE duplication, this engine's stated direction. Not a U.9 regression (pre-fix BOTH survived, which was strictly worse) and not wrong money in the double-count sense, but the ranking claims to pick "the side whose ownership window most tightly contains the date" while ignoring `feedDroppedAt`, which already travels with this data one layer up. Two instrumented runs proved: ~40 specs' direct better-sqlite3 connections commit seeds to the single shared e2e file; a commit landing between a server action's first read and first write dooms its write upgrade to a FULL 15s busy_timeout burn (BUSY_SNAPSHOT never clears for that snapshot); the Prisma engine serializes per connection, so burns stack — 97 POSTs never resolved in one run, 6–19.4s+ action latencies, 3 tests failing on 20–30s response timeouts, and the ledger's 12 CI reads across 8 tests.
